# copied unchanged. Boots 17:02:44 (robot handled before START: 8 LIFT events, no driving) and 17:03:21,
# 17:04:23, 17:06:10 (no START) are left out.
#
# No pass list was written down; the boots below are identified from the log (Claude, 23 Nov):
#   17:07:14  START, ~2.2 m of line following (26k ticks), PAUSE, then handled while paused: one
#             `hump entry` + `EVENT HUMP height=unavailable (no wheel distance)` from the handling.
#   17:09:43  three ramp passes in one boot, PAUSE/RESUME between them:
#             pass 1: entry (peak 97 mg), robot STALLED on the ramp, `hump timed out` (HT#1)
#             pass 2: entry (peak 113 mg), STALLED again, timed out (HT#2); then S5 HOLD (obstacle 54 mm)
#             pass 3: entry (peak 93 mg), drove over: `EVENT HUMP height=11 mm` (HT#3)
#   17:13:24, 17:14:14, 17:14:57  ~1 m each (11-12k ticks), PAUSE: no hump activity (flat runs)
#   17:16:18  START, lifted while following: `EVENT LIFT`, `stopped: robot lifted (IMU)`, stays stopped
#
# Findings:
# 1. Both timeouts are the robot stalling on the ramp, not the detector: on the ramp the right line
#    sensor reads the line on every sample (`steer ... R=473 ... act right=473`), steering sets the
#    right wheel to 0 (`PWM=13/0`), the left wheel alone cannot move it and the ticks freeze
#    (L=5028 R=5114; L=9846 R=10036) until the 1.5 s timeout. Same stall as the 20 Nov runs.
# 2. The one pass that drove over read 11 mm against 10 mm with the current factor (25/11).
#    Wheel distance was 2-3 mm per 20 ms sample (cruise PWM 13-15/17-21 %).
# 3. Climb signal: filtered forward 55-100 mg on the ramp; about 10 samples at 50-90 mg come before
#    `hump entry` (85 mg x 3) and are not counted in the height.
# 4. Flat-floor noise while driving (93 one-second `HT noise` windows): filtered f -151..122 mg,
#    mean |f| 23.9 mg; raw -324..274 mg. 27 windows had a filtered spike >= 85 mg (the entry
#    threshold) but none lasted 3 samples: 0 hump entries on flat floor.
# 5. Cycle time: max 14 ms over 2188 traced samples, none over 20 ms.
